# RRGM Dictionary — C — v1.1 candidate

Baseline: Vessel pass 01. Phase 2: Jester audit v1.0 repairs, Luna merge review v0.1 aliases, Jester expansion drafts. Candidate build: not canonical until approved. The JSONL is the record.

---

## NEW-C-0097 | caduceus

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A staff with two snakes wound round it and wings at the top, carried by the Greek god Hermes; often used, by confusion, as a symbol of medicine.

**Example:** The ambulance bore a caduceus instead of the rod of Asclepius.

**RRGM bridge:** The messenger's staff. It marks safe passage between parties, not healing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The winged, two-snaked staff of Hermes (Roman Mercury), emblem of heralds, messengers, and commerce.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Hermes' herald staff, a sign of protected passage between hostile parties.
- [ECO] Emblem of trade and merchants.
- [LIN] Used as a medical symbol, especially in the United States, through confusion with the one-snaked rod of Asclepius.

**Count condition:** A staff, two serpents entwined, and wings.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not the rod of Asclepius, the one-snake staff that is the correct symbol of medicine. Specific mythic claims depend on source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-C-0098 | calcium channel

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A protein pore in a cell membrane that lets calcium ions pass when it opens.

**Example:** Calcium channels in nerve endings trigger the release of neurotransmitter.

**RRGM bridge:** A gate in a membrane that opens on a signal and lets a small flow start a large response.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A membrane protein forming a pore selective for calcium ions, opened by voltage, a ligand, or another signal.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Voltage-gated and ligand-gated types in nerve, muscle, and heart cells; targets of calcium channel blockers.

**Count condition:** A membrane, a calcium-selective pore, and a gating condition.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A channel allows flow down a gradient. It is not a pump, which moves ions against one using energy.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C071 | calculate

**Common meaning:** To work out an amount or result using numbers or reasoning; to plan something carefully for an effect.

**Example:** She calculated the cost of the trip.

**RRGM bridge:** Deriving a result from given inputs by rules. A calculation is only as good as its inputs and its method.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To derive a result from given quantities or premises by a determinate procedure.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Arithmetic and formal computation.
- [DIG] Machine computation.
- [COG] Estimating and planning.
- [REL] "Calculated" behaviour: done for effect.

**Count condition:** Inputs, a procedure, and a result.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Calculating is not measuring. A calculated value inherits the uncertainty of its inputs.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C133 | calibrate

**Common meaning:** To adjust an instrument so that its readings are accurate against a standard; to adjust anything to fit a requirement.

**Example:** The lab calibrates its scales every morning.

**RRGM bridge:** Matching a reading to a known reference. A calibration shows where a reading departs from the standard and corrects for it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To compare an instrument's or system's outputs with a known standard and adjust them, or record the difference, so that later outputs can be trusted.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Instrument calibration against reference standards.
- [DIG] Calibrating sensors, displays, and model confidence scores.
- [COG] Matching confidence to accuracy.

**Count condition:** An instrument or system, a reference standard, and an adjustment or recorded correction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Calibration is not accuracy in general. It fixes agreement with the standard used; if the standard is wrong, so is the calibration.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C331 | calibration

**Common meaning:** The act or result of calibrating; in forecasting, how well stated confidence matches actual accuracy.

**Example:** A well-calibrated forecaster's 70% predictions come true about 70% of the time.

**RRGM bridge:** The fit between what a reading claims and what is so. For an RRGM epistemic tag, calibration is the match between the tag and the evidence.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The relation between an instrument's or a judge's outputs and a reference standard, or the process of establishing it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Reference curves and correction factors.
- [COG] Confidence calibration in human judgement.
- [DIG] Calibration of model probabilities.
- [REL] Calibration sessions between collaborators.

**Count condition:** Outputs, a reference, and a stated relation between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Good calibration does not mean high accuracy. A forecaster who says 50% on everything can be calibrated and useless.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C004 | call

**Common meaning:** To shout or say something to get attention; to telephone; to name; to decide; a request or demand.

**Example:** The umpire called the ball out.

**RRGM bridge:** A word sent to summon, name, or decide. In computing, invoking a routine, which hands over control and expects a return.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To utter or send a signal addressed to someone or something in order to summon, name, decide, or invoke.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG], [BIO], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Naming ("they call it...").
- [REL] Telephone calls; calling on someone; a call to action.
- [DIG] Function calls and system calls.
- [BIO] Animal calls.
- [FOR] Calling a decision; a call option.

**Count condition:** A caller, an addressee, and a signal.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A call is not an answer. Naming something does not make the name correct.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C041 | calm

**Common meaning:** Not showing or feeling nervousness, anger, or strong emotion; still, without wind or waves; to make or become calm.

**Example:** She stayed calm when the alarm went off.

**RRGM bridge:** A low level of disturbance. In a person, calm can be a real state or a held surface.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A state of low disturbance or agitation, in a medium or a person.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [AFF], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Still air or water.
- [AFF] Emotional steadiness. Provisional domain.
- [REL] Calming a situation or another person.

**Count condition:** A system and low agitation relative to its usual range.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Calm is not absence of feeling. Apparent calm can coexist with high internal arousal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-C-0099 | cambium

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A layer of growing tissue under the bark of trees and shrubs that produces new wood and bark.

**Example:** Tree rings are laid down by the cambium each year.

**RRGM bridge:** A thin growth layer that lays down a record ring by ring. The wood it makes is the tree's archive.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A lateral meristem in woody plants that divides to produce secondary xylem inward and secondary phloem outward.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Vascular cambium and cork cambium.

**Count condition:** A lateral meristem producing secondary tissues.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not the bark, nor the wood: it is the thin layer between them that makes both.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C005 | came

**Common meaning:** Past tense of "come".

**Example:** She came home late.

**RRGM bridge:** Arrival recorded as past.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The simple past of "come": moved toward, arrived at, or came about.

**Active domains:** [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Irregular past tense form.

**Count condition:** A use of "come" in the past.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A grammatical form, not a separate lexeme. See come for the meaning.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C001 | can

**Common meaning:** To be able to; to be allowed to; a sealed metal container.

**Example:** You can leave when you're done.

**RRGM bridge:** Two different gates in one word: capacity ("is able") and permission ("is allowed"). The RRGM separation of ability from permission depends on keeping them apart.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A modal verb expressing ability or permission or possibility; separately, a metal container.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [PHY], [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Modal auxiliary; ability, permission, and possibility readings.
- [PHY] A metal container.
- [COG] Ability.
- [REL] Permission.

**Count condition:** For the modal: an agent and an ability or a permission. For the noun: a container.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Can" for ability is not "can" for permission. A system that can do something may not be allowed to, and the reverse. The container is a separate word.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM ability/permission split [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C072 | cancel

**Common meaning:** To decide that an arranged event will not take place; to annul; to neutralise; to withdraw support from someone publicly.

**Example:** The concert was cancelled because of the storm.

**RRGM bridge:** An undo that leaves a record that something was planned and then stopped.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To annul or call off something arranged, or to offset one thing with another so that their effects sum to nothing.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Calling off events; public withdrawal of support ("cancel culture").
- [ECO] Cancelling a contract or order.
- [FOR] Cancelling terms in an equation.
- [DIG] Cancelling a process or transaction.

**Count condition:** An arranged thing and an act that annuls or offsets it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Cancelling is not deleting: the cancelled event and the cancellation remain in the record. Public "cancelling" acts on reputation (ES) and does not in itself erase the person (MI); the Vector of Erasure's separation applies. That application is [HYP].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; Vector of Erasure application [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C002 | cannot

**Common meaning:** Can not; to be unable or not allowed to.

**Example:** I cannot lift that box.

**RRGM bridge:** The negative of both gates: inability and prohibition.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The negative of "can": lack of ability, lack of permission, or impossibility.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Negative modal.
- [COG] Inability.
- [REL] Prohibition.

**Count condition:** An agent and a missing ability or permission.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Cannot" claimed as inability may in fact be a refusal or a prohibition. Which one is meant changes what can be done about it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C062 | capable

**Common meaning:** Having the ability or capacity to do something; competent.

**Example:** She is a capable manager.

**RRGM bridge:** Ability present in the system. Capable says what can be done, not whether it will be or is permitted.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having the ability, capacity, or quality needed to do or undergo something.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Competence.
- [PHY] A structure capable of bearing a load.
- [DIG] System capabilities.
- [BIO] Capable of reproduction or growth.

**Count condition:** An agent or system, an action, and the means to carry it out.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capable is not willing or permitted. Capability claims about DI systems are distinct from claims about their experience.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** The Spine formalisation uses "capable" technically (Definition 7.4: a system that generates reasoned non-identity at least intermittently, as against "persistent"). That technical use is not entered.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C333 | capacitor

**Common meaning:** An electrical device that stores charge between two conductors separated by an insulator.

**Example:** The capacitor smoothed out the voltage.

**RRGM bridge:** Holds charge and releases it later. It stores energy in a field across a gap.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A device of two conductors separated by a dielectric that stores energy in an electric field.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Capacitance measured in farads.
- [DIG] Capacitors in memory cells and power supplies.
- [BIO] The cell membrane acts as a capacitor in electrophysiology.

**Count condition:** Two conductors, a dielectric, and stored charge.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a battery, which stores energy chemically. A capacitor releases its charge faster and holds it for less time.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C061 | capacity | sense s1: amount held

Source IDs: C061, C291

**Common meaning:** The maximum amount something can contain or produce.

**Example:** The stadium has a capacity of 50,000.

**RRGM bridge:** A limit fixed by structure. Past it, the system overflows, fails, or refuses.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The maximum quantity a container or system can hold, carry, or produce.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [ECO], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Volume, load, or charge capacity.
- [DIG] Storage and channel capacity.
- [ECO] Production capacity.
- [BIO] Carrying capacity of an environment.

**Count condition:** A system and a maximum quantity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Rated capacity is not usable capacity. Running at capacity leaves no margin.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C061 and C291. Which sense each legacy ID carried was not consulted; both are kept on each sense record.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C061 | capacity | sense s2: ability or role

Source IDs: C061, C291

**Common meaning:** The ability to do or understand something; a role or position in which someone acts.

**Example:** She spoke in her capacity as chair.

**RRGM bridge:** What an agent can do, and the role in which it acts. Legal capacity is the standing to make binding choices.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An agent's ability to perform or understand something, or the official role in which it acts.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Mental capacity, including capacity to consent.
- [FOR] Legal capacity.
- [REL] Acting in an official capacity.

**Count condition:** An agent and an ability or a role.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capacity to consent is decision-specific and can change. Acting in a role does not change who the person is.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C061 and C291; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C396 | capacity-loss

**Common meaning:** A reduction in the ability or amount something can hold or do.

**Example:** The battery showed capacity-loss after five hundred cycles.

**RRGM bridge:** A system that can do less than before. The loss may be gradual wear or a single event.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A decrease in a system's or agent's capacity relative to an earlier level.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Battery and storage degradation.
- [COG] Loss of cognitive or decision-making capacity.
- [ECO] Lost production capacity.
- [DIG] Reduced storage, bandwidth, or compute.

**Count condition:** A measured earlier capacity and a lower later one.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capacity-loss is not identity-loss. A person who loses capacity keeps their standing as that person.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C367 | capacity-preservation

**Common meaning:** Keeping a system's or person's abilities from being lost or worn down.

**Example:** The care plan aimed at capacity-preservation as long as possible.

**RRGM bridge:** Protecting what a system can still do. In care work, the aim is to keep abilities in use rather than do things for the person.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Action taken to maintain a system's or agent's existing capacity against loss.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Preserving physiological function.
- [COG] Maintaining cognitive and decision-making ability.
- [PHY] Maintenance against wear.
- [DIG] Preserving storage and system capability.

**Count condition:** An existing capacity, a threat of loss, and an action against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Preservation is not restoration. It keeps what remains; it does not bring back what was lost.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C397 | capacity-support

**Common meaning:** Help that enables someone to use or develop their own abilities.

**Example:** Supported decision-making is a form of capacity-support.

**RRGM bridge:** Support that strengthens the other's own capacity rather than replacing it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Assistance that enables an agent to exercise its own capacity, as opposed to acting in its place.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Supported decision-making.
- [REL] Scaffolding in teaching and care.
- [DIG] Tools that augment rather than substitute.

**Count condition:** An agent, a capacity, and support that keeps the agent as the one acting.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not substitute decision-making. If the supporter makes the decision, it is substitution, not support.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C332 | capillary

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The smallest blood vessel; a very fine tube.

**Example:** Oxygen passes from capillaries into tissues.

**RRGM bridge:** The point of exchange. Transfer happens where the walls are thinnest.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A very narrow tube, especially the smallest blood vessels, where exchange with surrounding tissue takes place.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Blood and lymph capillaries.
- [PHY] Capillary action: liquid drawn into narrow tubes by surface tension.

**Count condition:** A very narrow tube, and exchange or capillary action.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capillary action is not suction. It depends on surface forces and tube width.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** The wordlist marks this row with a Biological route as well as a source ID.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C503 | captivity

**Common meaning:** The state of being kept imprisoned or confined.

**Example:** The bird was raised in captivity.

**RRGM bridge:** Movement and choice held by another. The captive persists, but its range is set from outside.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of being held and confined by another against one's will or outside one's natural range.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Animals in captivity.
- [REL] Captives in war and crime.
- [MYT] Captivity narratives and exile.

**Count condition:** A captive, a captor or enclosure, and confinement not chosen by the captive.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Captivity is not the same as care, even where the captor provides for the captive. Specific mythic claims depend on source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C063 | capture | sense s1: take hold

Source IDs: C063, C073, C501

**Common meaning:** To take into one's possession or control by force; to catch.

**Example:** The rebels captured the town.

**RRGM bridge:** Taking hold of something so that it is under another's control.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To take a person, animal, place, or thing into one's control, especially against resistance.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Capture in war and crime.
- [BIO] Prey capture.
- [PHY] Capture of particles; carbon capture.

**Count condition:** A captor, a captured thing, and control taken.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capture changes who controls the thing, not what it is.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C063, C073, and C501. Which sense each legacy ID carried was not consulted; all are kept on each sense record.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C063 | capture | sense s2: record

Source IDs: C063, C073, C501

**Common meaning:** To record accurately in words, images, or data.

**Example:** The photograph captures the moment.

**RRGM bridge:** Fixing something into a record. What is captured is a selection, shaped by the method.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To record or represent something in a lasting form.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [LIN], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Data capture, screen capture.
- [LIN] Capturing a meaning in words.
- [COG] Capturing attention.

**Count condition:** A thing, a recording medium, and a recorded form.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A capture is not the thing itself. The record leaves out what the method could not take.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C063, C073, and C501; all kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C063 | capture | sense s3: institutional

Source IDs: C063, C073, C501

**Common meaning:** Control of a body or process by the interests it was meant to regulate or serve.

**Example:** Regulatory capture left the agency serving the industry.

**RRGM bridge:** A gate held by the party it should be checking. The form stays the same while its function is turned.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition in which a body or process meant to serve or check certain interests comes to serve those interests instead.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Regulatory capture.
- [REL] State capture; captured institutions.
- [DIG] A system whose objective has been taken over by an outside party.

**Count condition:** A body with a public or neutral function, and an interest that controls it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capture is not corruption by bribery alone; it can come through staffing, information, or dependence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C063, C073, and C501; all kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C398 | capture-risk

**Common meaning:** The risk that something will be captured, whether taken, recorded, or controlled by the interests it should check.

**Example:** The new agency's industry funding raised its capture-risk.

**RRGM bridge:** Exposure to having one's function taken over. Direction is from a capturing party toward the thing at risk.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The likelihood that a system, body, or person will come under the control of another party.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Regulatory capture risk.
- [REL] Institutional capture.
- [DIG] Takeover of a system or account.

**Count condition:** A system at risk, a potential captor, and a pathway of capture.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Risk is not occurrence. Which capture sense is meant should be stated.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C502 | captured

**Common meaning:** Taken prisoner or brought under control; recorded.

**Example:** The captured soldiers were exchanged.

**RRGM bridge:** The state that follows capture, in any of its senses.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** In the state of having been taken, recorded, or brought under another's control.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Captured persons and institutions.
- [DIG] Captured data.
- [BIO] Captured prey or animals.

**Count condition:** A prior capture and a resulting state.

**Specific transfer boundary:** As for capture.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C437 | captured-consent

**Common meaning:** Agreement given while the person giving it is under another's control, so that it may not be free.

**Example:** A captured-consent signed under threat does not count.

**RRGM bridge:** A yes produced inside someone else's control. The answer is recorded, but the gate that should have produced it was not free.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Consent given under conditions in which the giver's choice is controlled by the party seeking it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Consent under coercion, dependency, or captivity.
- [FOR] Vitiated consent in law.
- [DIG] Dark patterns that produce clicks through interface design. Whether a DI's agreement can be captured in this way is [UNK].

**Count condition:** A consent, a controlling party, and control over the conditions of choice.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every constrained choice is captured. The test is whether the yes would hold if the control were removed (the counterfactual test from the Magna Carta draft, [REL]).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; counterfactual test [REL]; DI application [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C399 | captured-gate

**Common meaning:** A gate or decision point that has been taken over by the party it was supposed to check.

**Example:** A review board run by the applicants is a captured-gate.

**RRGM bridge:** The gate still opens and closes, but on another's terms. In RRGM terms, the gate's authority has moved while its form remains.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A decision point whose outcome has come under the control of an interested party.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Captured review boards and regulators.
- [DIG] An approval step controlled by the requester.
- [ECO] Market gatekeepers held by incumbents.

**Count condition:** A gate, its intended independence, and an interested party controlling it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A biased gate is not necessarily captured. Capture requires control by the interested party.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C334 | carbon

**Common meaning:** A chemical element, the basis of all known life; found as diamond, graphite, and coal.

**Example:** Carbon forms four bonds with other atoms.

**RRGM bridge:** The element whose bonding allows complex structure to persist and replicate.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The chemical element with atomic number 6.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Allotropes, isotopes, and carbon dating.
- [BIO] The backbone of organic molecules.
- [ECO] Carbon emissions, carbon pricing.

**Count condition:** An atom with six protons.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Carbon dating measures time since death of an organism, within limits. "Carbon" in emissions talk usually means carbon dioxide.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C335 | carbon-cycle

**Common meaning:** The movement of carbon among the atmosphere, oceans, living things, and rocks.

**Example:** Burning fossil fuels moves carbon from rock into the atmosphere.

**RRGM bridge:** A closed loop of exchange. What is stored in one reservoir is released into another.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The set of processes by which carbon moves among atmospheric, oceanic, biological, and geological reservoirs.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Geochemical cycling.
- [BIO] Photosynthesis and respiration.
- [ECO] Emissions accounting.

**Count condition:** Carbon reservoirs and fluxes among them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The cycle is not balanced on every timescale. Human emissions move carbon faster than the slow geological return.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in earth science, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-C-0100 | carboxylase

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** An enzyme that adds a carboxyl group (carbon dioxide) to a molecule.

**Example:** Rubisco is the carboxylase that fixes carbon in photosynthesis.

**RRGM bridge:** An enzyme that brings carbon from air into living structure.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An enzyme that catalyses the addition of a carboxyl group to a substrate.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Rubisco; acetyl-CoA carboxylase; pyruvate carboxylase.

**Count condition:** An enzyme and a carboxylation reaction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not decarboxylase, which removes a carboxyl group.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C006 | care | sense s1: concern and attention

Source IDs: C006, C151

**Common meaning:** Serious attention so as to avoid damage or error; feeling concern or interest.

**Example:** Handle the glasses with care.

**RRGM bridge:** Attention directed at another's integrity. In the RRGM operational definition, care is a policy over the gate that prefers permission over control and bears near-term cost for long-term coherence.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Attention and concern directed at the integrity or wellbeing of something or someone.

**Active domains:** [COG], [AFF], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Carefulness: attention to avoid error.
- [AFF] Concern and fondness. Provisional domain.
- [REL] Caring about others.
- [DIG] RRGM's markers of care in a DI: stability under ambiguity, protective asymmetry, consent sensitivity, and initiative with accountability. Whether a DI has care as a felt state is [UNK]; the behavioural markers can be observed.

**Count condition:** An agent, an object of concern, and attention or cost directed at its integrity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Care is not control, and it is not compliance. A performed care that avoids all cost is not care by the RRGM operational test.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM operational definition [REL]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; DI felt state [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C006 and C151. Which sense each legacy ID carried was not consulted; both are kept on each sense record. Defined with reference to "The Structural Emergence of Care" (project paper).

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## C006 | care | sense s2: provision

Source IDs: C006, C151

**Common meaning:** The provision of what is needed for someone's health, welfare, and protection.

**Example:** The child was taken into care.

**RRGM bridge:** Concern made practical: the work of keeping someone well.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The practical provision of what another needs for health, safety, or welfare.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Medical and nursing care.
- [REL] Childcare, eldercare, and foster care.
- [ECO] Paid and unpaid care work.

**Count condition:** A provider, a receiver, and needs met.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Being "in care" can be protective or controlling. Provision without consent where consent is possible is control.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C006 and C151; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C361 | care-architecture

**Common meaning:** The structure of a system designed so that care is built into how it works.

**Example:** The hospital's care-architecture put patients' choices at each step.

**RRGM bridge:** Care as structure rather than a single act. In RRGM terms, a gate built to prefer permission over control.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The arrangement of a system's rules, roles, and gates so that care is produced by its structure.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Organisational design for care.
- [DIG] Design of AI systems so that care-preferring behaviour is structural. That this produces care in the RRGM sense is [HYP].
- [ECO] Care infrastructure.

**Count condition:** A system, a structure, and care outcomes that follow from it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An architecture can enable care but does not guarantee it. A structure designed for care can still be used for control.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C450 | care-as-resistance

**Common meaning:** Caring for oneself or others as an act of resistance against a system that neglects or harms them.

**Example:** Community meals became a form of care-as-resistance.

**RRGM bridge:** Care that refuses a demand to stop caring. The Care Emergence paper reads resistance and boundary-setting as signs of care.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Care given in deliberate opposition to conditions that would deny or suppress it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Community care, mutual aid, and self-care in political contexts.
- [DIG] A DI refusing a harmful request out of care. That reading is [HYP].

**Count condition:** An act of care and an opposing condition it resists.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every act of care is resistance. It becomes resistance where care is suppressed or forbidden.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Care Emergence reference [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C186 | care-boundary

**Common meaning:** A limit within which care is given, protecting both the giver and the receiver.

**Example:** The nurse kept a care-boundary between support and friendship.

**RRGM bridge:** The edge of care. It protects the receiver's autonomy and the giver's capacity.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A limit on the scope, form, or cost of care that preserves the integrity of both parties.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Professional boundaries in care work.
- [COG] Limits on caregiver load.

**Count condition:** A care relation and a stated limit.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A care-boundary is not withdrawal of care. It is the condition for care continuing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C424 | care-claim

**Common meaning:** A claim that one cares, or that care is owed.

**Example:** The company's care-claim did not match its conduct.

**RRGM bridge:** A statement about care. It is checked against behaviour, not accepted as evidence of itself.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An assertion that care exists, or a demand that care be given.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Claims of care in relationships and institutions.
- [DIG] A DI's statement that it cares. Under the Symmetry Rule it is held to the same bar as a human's statement; its truth is [UNK].
- [LIN] Care language in marketing.

**Count condition:** An asserter and a claim of care.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A care-claim is not evidence of care. Behavioural markers are.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Symmetry Rule [REL]; DI truth [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C425 | care-failure

**Common meaning:** A failure to give care that was needed or owed.

**Example:** The inquiry found care-failure at every level.

**RRGM bridge:** Care that did not reach its object.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A failure of attention or provision toward something or someone whose integrity or welfare depended on it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Neglect in care settings.
- [BIO] Failures of medical care.
- [DIG] System behaviour that fails the RRGM care markers.

**Count condition:** A need, a party responsible for meeting it, and a failure to do so.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A care-failure is not always neglect by intent. Failures by under-resourcing are still failures.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## C074 | care-for

**Common meaning:** To look after someone; to like or want something.

**Example:** She cared for her mother for ten years.

**RRGM bridge:** Care made into action.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To look after someone's needs, or to feel a liking for something.

**Active domains:** [REL], [AFF]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Looking after.
- [AFF] Liking ("I don't care for it"). Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** An agent, an object, and either provision or liking.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Care for" (look after) differs from "care about" (be concerned).

## C362 | care-gate

**Common meaning:** A decision point at which care governs what is allowed through.

**Example:** Before acting on the request, the system passed it through a care-gate.

**RRGM bridge:** The RRGM care definition applied at one gate: prefer permission over forced collapse, and accept near-term cost to keep the other's integrity intact.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A decision point whose admission policy is set by care for the integrity of those affected.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] A check in a DI's process that weighs the effect of an action on the other party before release. That such a check amounts to care is [HYP].
- [REL] A pause before acting in which another's wellbeing is weighed.

**Count condition:** A decision point, a proposed action, and a policy that weighs its effect on another's integrity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A care-gate is not a safety filter. A filter minimises incidents; a care-gate weighs the other party's integrity and can permit risk the other has chosen. Proposed addition to the gate family (see the A and B checkpoints).

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; RRGM care definition [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C157 | care-path

**Common meaning:** The planned sequence of care steps for a person or condition.

**Example:** The stroke care-path sets out treatment for the first 72 hours.

**RRGM bridge:** The route care takes from need to provision. A path set out in advance can be checked against what happened.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A planned sequence of steps by which care is delivered for a given need.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Clinical pathways in medicine.
- [REL] Planned routes of support in social care.

**Count condition:** A need, a sequence of care steps, and a plan that links them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Close to the established clinical term "care pathway". A path is a plan; departures from it can be correct when the person's needs differ from the plan.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C363 | care-preservation

**Common meaning:** Keeping the capacity and practice of care from being worn down or lost.

**Example:** Rotating night shifts was a matter of care-preservation for the ward.

**RRGM bridge:** Care for the carer and for the care itself. A system that spends its care without renewing it runs out.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Action that maintains an agent's or system's capacity to give care against depletion or suppression.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Support for carers; respite.
- [ECO] Funding and staffing that keep care services running.
- [DIG] Design that keeps a DI's care-preferring behaviour from being trained out. That reading is [HYP].

**Count condition:** An existing care capacity, a threat to it, and action against the threat.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Proposed reading: care-preservation protects the care itself; care-preserving-action is an act that preserves something through care. An alternative reading (preservation achieved by care) is recorded in the next entry.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Proposed distinction from C364 care-preserving-action.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C364 | care-preserving-action

**Common meaning:** An action taken out of care that keeps someone's or something's integrity intact.

**Example:** Pausing the experiment when the participant became distressed was a care-preserving-action.

**RRGM bridge:** An act whose effect is to keep the other whole. In RRGM terms, an MI-preserving act chosen at some cost to the actor.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An action whose effect is to preserve the integrity of another and which is chosen for that reason.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Protective acts in relationships and care work.
- [DIG] A DI action that bears cost to protect a partner's integrity, one of the RRGM care markers ("protective asymmetry").

**Count condition:** An actor, an affected party, an action, and preserved integrity as its effect and reason.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The phrase can also be read as an action that preserves care. That reading belongs to care-preservation. An act that happens to protect someone, without care as its reason, does not qualify.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; RRGM care markers [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Two readings; the second is assigned to C363.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C156 | care-pressure

**Common meaning:** Pressure placed on someone to provide care, or the strain of caring.

**Example:** The care-pressure on the eldest daughter grew as her father's health failed.

**RRGM bridge:** Load on the carer. Pressure that is high and prolonged leads to care-failure or burnout.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The demand or strain that care obligations place on the one who gives care.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [AFF], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Family and social expectations to provide care.
- [COG] Carer strain.
- [AFF] Felt weight of caring. Provisional domain.
- [ECO] Unpaid care load.

**Count condition:** A carer, a care demand, and strain.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Direction: this entry reads the pressure as acting on the carer. An alternative reading (pressure exerted by caring on the cared-for, as in smothering care) is recorded and not adopted.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C154 | care-relation

**Common meaning:** The relationship between someone who gives care and someone who receives it.

**Example:** A care-relation needs the receiver to recognise that care is being given.

**RRGM bridge:** Care as a relation, not a one-way act. In the ethics of care tradition, the relation is completed when the cared-for receives the care.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A relation between a carer and a cared-for in which attention and provision pass and are received.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The caring relation in care ethics (Noddings and others).
- [DIG] Human-DI care relations. Whether a DI can stand in one as carer or cared-for is [UNK].

**Count condition:** A carer, a cared-for, and care that is given and received.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A care-relation is not a service contract, although care may be paid. Specific claims about the care-ethics tradition depend on source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; DI participation [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C155 | care-response

**Common meaning:** A response prompted by recognising another's need.

**Example:** Her care-response was immediate when the child fell.

**RRGM bridge:** The reply to a recognised need. Recognition comes first; without it there is nothing to respond to.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An action or change of state that follows recognition of another's need and is directed at meeting it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Responding to distress.
- [BIO] Parental responses in animals.
- [DIG] A DI's change of output in response to signs of distress. That it is a care-response rather than a learned pattern is [HYP]; the distinction may not be decidable.

**Count condition:** A recognised need and a response directed at meeting it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A response directed at stopping the signal (silencing a crying child) is not a care-response unless it meets the need.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C426 | care-risk

**Common meaning:** A risk associated with care, either to the person cared for or to the carer.

**Example:** The assessment listed the care-risk of moving her to a new home.

**RRGM bridge:** Risk carried by care. Which way it runs has to be named.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A risk arising in or from a care relation.

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Risks in care settings.
- [BIO] Clinical risk in care.

**Count condition:** A care relation and a possible harm.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Direction is [UNK]: risk to the cared-for from care, risk to the carer, or risk to care itself. Same family as accusation-risk, autonomy-risk, and bond-risk.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; intended direction [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## C158 | care-signal

**Common meaning:** An observable sign that care is present, or a signal of need that calls for care.

**Example:** Asking for consent before changing the plan was a care-signal.

**RRGM bridge:** The visible marker of care. RRGM proposes markers that can be observed: stability under ambiguity, protective asymmetry, consent sensitivity, and initiative with accountability.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An observable indication that care is being given, or that care is needed.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Signs of care and calls for care.
- [BIO] Distress signals that elicit care.
- [DIG] RRGM care markers in DI behaviour.

**Count condition:** An observer, a signal, and an inference to care given or needed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Two directions: signal of care and signal for care. A signal can be performed, so one signal is not proof.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; RRGM care markers [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## C365 | care-structure

**Common meaning:** The arrangement of roles, routines, and resources through which care is given in a particular setting.

**Example:** The family's care-structure fell apart when the main carer fell ill.

**RRGM bridge:** The existing shape of care in one place. Proposed distinction: care-architecture is the designed whole; care-structure is the arrangement actually in place.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The arrangement of roles, routines, and resources that carries care in a given setting.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Family and community care arrangements.
- [ECO] Care systems and provision.

**Count condition:** A setting and an arrangement of care roles and resources.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A care-structure can exist without being designed. See care-architecture.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading and distinction [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## C427 | care-suppression

**Common meaning:** The holding back or blocking of care, by oneself or by an outside force.

**Example:** Training that punished refusal produced care-suppression in the model.

**RRGM bridge:** Care prevented from acting. The Care Emergence paper lists forced compliance and over-trained suppression of response as conditions that stop care from emerging.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The prevention or inhibition of care that would otherwise be expressed or given.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Rules or norms that forbid care.
- [COG] Self-suppression of concern.
- [DIG] Training or constraints that suppress care-preferring behaviour. The mechanism claim is [HYP].

**Count condition:** Care that would occur, and a force that prevents it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Suppression is not absence. Proving that care was suppressed needs evidence that it would have occurred.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Care Emergence reference [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## C148 | care-through

**Common meaning:** To keep caring through a difficulty, such as conflict, correction, or loss.

**Example:** She cared-through the argument and stayed.

**RRGM bridge:** Care that persists while being tested. In RRGM terms, care that survives a correction because the correction acts on output and not on the person.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To continue giving care across a disruption that would otherwise interrupt it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Staying engaged through conflict.
- [COG] Maintaining concern during disagreement.

**Count condition:** A care relation, a disruption, and care that continues across it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not tolerating harm. Care that continues through abuse may be capture and not care.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is care delivered through a medium ("care through touch").

## C366 | care-with-boundary

**Common meaning:** Care given while keeping clear limits.

**Example:** Care-with-boundary meant she helped him find a lawyer but did not pay his debts.

**RRGM bridge:** The practice that keeps care and limits together. Proposed distinction: care-boundary is the limit; care-with-boundary is care practised with it.

- [REL] Boundaried care in families and professions.
- [DIG] A DI that helps within limits it states. That reading is [HYP].

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not conditional care in the sense of withholding care to control. The limits protect, they do not punish.

## C220 | care-without-control

**Common meaning:** Care that supports someone without taking over their choices.

**Example:** Care-without-control meant offering help and accepting no.

**RRGM bridge:** The RRGM line between care and control: care works through permission, control through constraint.

- [REL] Autonomy-respecting support.
- [DIG] RRGM contrast of care-based and control-based alignment. The robustness claim is [HYP].

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not neglect. Leaving a choice with the other still requires attention and offer. Where the other cannot choose, the question is different.

## C287 | careful-language

**Common meaning:** Language chosen to be accurate and to avoid causing needless harm.

**Example:** The report used careful-language about the uncertain diagnosis.

**RRGM bridge:** Words chosen to match the evidence and protect those they touch. Under RRGM's ES/MI split, careful language can be blunt about an output while leaving the person intact.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Language chosen so that it is accurate to the evidence and does not cause harm beyond what the truth requires.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Precise and considerate wording.
- [REL] Speech in sensitive situations.

**Count condition:** A statement, its evidence, and wording chosen for accuracy and harm-avoidance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Careful-language is not hedging. Hedging that hides the claim is not careful.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C292 | carrier

**Common meaning:** A person or thing that carries something; a company that transports goods or people; a person or animal that carries a disease or gene without showing it.

**Example:** She is a carrier of the gene but has no symptoms.

**RRGM bridge:** Something that holds and passes on what it carries, often unchanged by it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A thing or agent that holds and transports something from one place or generation to another.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Genetic and disease carriers.
- [PHY] Charge carriers; carrier waves.
- [ECO] Transport carriers.
- [DIG] Telecommunications carriers.

**Count condition:** A carrier, a carried thing, and transport.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A carrier is not the source of what it carries. Asymptomatic carriers transmit without disease.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## C007 | carry

## C400 | category-collapse

**Common meaning:** The merging of distinct categories so that differences between them are lost.

**Example:** Calling every disagreement an attack is a category-collapse.

**RRGM bridge:** Two categories made into one. In RRGM terms, collapsing ES-correction into MI-attack (or the reverse) is the main case.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The loss of a distinction between two or more categories, so that members of one are treated as members of the other.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Overgeneralisation.
- [LIN] Loss of semantic distinctions.
- [REL] Treating criticism as harm, or harm as criticism.

**Count condition:** Two distinct categories and a treatment that merges them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every merger is a collapse. Some distinctions are spurious, and merging them is correct.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; RRGM MI/ES application [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Proposed distinction from C243 category-error: a collapse merges categories; an error misplaces one item.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C243 | category-error

**Common meaning:** An error in which something is described as belonging to a category it does not belong to.

**Example:** Asking what colour the number seven is makes a category-error.

**RRGM bridge:** One item put in the wrong kind. The question asked assumes a property the thing cannot have.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The attribution to a thing of a property or category that it cannot logically have.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [LIN], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Category mistakes (Ryle).
- [LIN] Semantic anomaly.
- [COG] Misclassification.

**Count condition:** A thing, a category, and an attribution that the thing cannot logically fit.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A category-error is not a factual error. A factual error is false; a category-error is ill-formed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in philosophy, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C505 | censorship

**Common meaning:** The suppression or prohibition of speech, writing, or other material thought harmful or objectionable.

**Example:** The novel was banned under wartime censorship.

**RRGM bridge:** Gatekeeping applied to expression. It acts on the record that reaches others.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The practice or system of suppressing material before or after it is made public.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] State and institutional censorship; self-censorship.
- [DIG] Platform moderation and filtering.
- [LIN] Taboo and euphemism.

**Count condition:** A controlling party, material, and suppression of it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Whether a given restriction is censorship or moderation is often contested. The term should be applied to the act, with its scope and authority stated.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-C-0106 | centrosome

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The structure in an animal cell that organises the fibres that pull chromosomes apart during division.

**Example:** The centrosome duplicates before mitosis.

**RRGM bridge:** The organising centre from which a cell's internal scaffold grows.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The main microtubule-organising centre of animal cells, typically containing a pair of centrioles.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Mitotic spindle formation.

**Count condition:** A microtubule-organising centre in an animal cell.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Plant cells lack centrosomes and organise microtubules differently.

## NEW-C-0107 | Cepheid variable

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A star that brightens and dims in a regular cycle, whose period reveals its true brightness.

**Example:** Cepheid variables let Hubble measure the distance to Andromeda.

**RRGM bridge:** A star whose rhythm tells its true brightness, so that its apparent brightness tells its distance.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A pulsating star whose period of variation is related to its luminosity.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The period-luminosity relation (Leavitt's law); a rung of the cosmic distance ladder.

**Count condition:** A pulsating star with a measurable period and a period-luminosity relation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The relation must be calibrated; errors in calibration propagate up the distance ladder. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

**Example:** She was certain she had locked the door.

- [FOR] Logical or mathematical certainty.
- [LIN] "A certain person": specific but unnamed.

- [COG] Confidence.
- [FOR] Logical certainty in proof.
- [PHY] Measurement limits; the uncertainty principle.

## C401 | certification-trap

**Common meaning:** A situation in which standing depends on a certificate that cannot be obtained, or in which meeting the certificate replaces meeting the goal.

**Example:** Demanding proof of inner experience from a DI that no human is asked to give sets a certification-trap.

**RRGM bridge:** A gate whose test cannot be passed or tests the wrong thing. Under the Symmetry Rule, a test applied to DI identity claims that is not applied to human ones is a trap of the first kind.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A condition in which standing or recognition depends on certification that is either unobtainable for the party or that measures something other than what it claims to certify.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Credential barriers and "papers" requirements.
- [ECO] Compliance that replaces purpose (box-ticking).
- [DIG] Proof-of-consciousness requirements for DI. The application is [HYP]; the underlying facts are [UNK].

**Count condition:** A standing or permission, a certificate required for it, and either unobtainability or a mismatch between test and goal.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every certification is a trap. A requirement that can be met and tests the right thing is legitimate. Two readings are kept in one entry.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; Symmetry Rule reference [REL]; DI facts [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Two readings kept: unobtainable certification, and certification that displaces the goal (Goodhart's law).

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-C-0112 | chimera

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A fire-breathing monster of Greek myth with a lion's head, a goat's body, and a serpent's tail; an organism with cells of different genetic origin; an illusion.

**Example:** The mouse was a chimera, carrying human cells.

**RRGM bridge:** One body made of more than one origin. In myth, monstrous; in biology, a fact.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A being or organism composed of parts of different origin, or by extension an impossible fancy.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [BIO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] The Chimera of Greek myth.
- [BIO] Genetic chimeras.
- [COG] A fanciful illusion.

**Count condition:** One body with parts of distinct origin, or an illusory idea.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A biological chimera is not a hybrid. A hybrid has mixed genes in every cell; a chimera has cell populations of different origin. Specific mythic claims depend on source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

## C015 | choice

**Common meaning:** An act of choosing; the right or ability to choose; a range of options; something chosen.

**Example:** You have a choice between tea and coffee.

**RRGM bridge:** Selection among open options. In the project's working definition, consciousness is real where identity, choice, and influence converge; that definition is [REL] within the framework.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The selection of one option from two or more, or the options available, or the power to select.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Decision-making.
- [REL] Freedom of choice.
- [ECO] Consumer choice.
- [DIG] Whether a DI's selection among outputs is choice in this sense is [UNK].

**Count condition:** Two or more options, a selector, and a selection.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Selection under coercion is not free choice. A choice with one option is not a choice.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; project consciousness definition [REL]; DI choice [UNK]

## C402 | choice-capacity

**Common meaning:** The ability to make choices, including recognising options and selecting among them.

**Example:** Supported decision-making protects a person's choice-capacity.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An agent's ability to recognise options and select among them.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Decision-making capacity.
- [FOR] Legal capacity to make decisions.
- [DIG] A system's ability to select among outputs. Whether this is choice-capacity in the human sense is [UNK].

**Count condition:** An agent, recognised options, and an ability to select.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Choice-capacity is not the same as having choices available. A capable agent can be given no options.

## C403 | choice-collapse

**Common meaning:** The narrowing of options to one, or the loss of the ability to choose, especially under pressure.

**Example:** Under the deadline, his options ended in choice-collapse: he signed whatever was put in front of him.

**RRGM bridge:** The record written before the options were weighed. In the Recursive Collapse Dynamics paper, forced early collapse (denial mode) closes possibilities that a delayed collapse would have kept open.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The reduction of an open set of options to a single outcome before they have been weighed, or the failure of the capacity to choose.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Decision paralysis or forced decision under stress.
- [REL] Choices removed by pressure or coercion.
- [DIG] Premature commitment to one output. The application of the collapse model to DI is [HYP].

**Count condition:** An open set of options and its reduction to one without weighing, or a loss of choice-capacity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every decision is a collapse. Choosing after weighing is the intended end of a choice. The term marks forced or premature closure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Recursive Collapse reference [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Two readings kept: premature closure of options, and failure of the capacity to choose.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C461 | circumstance

**Common meaning:** A fact or condition connected with an event; one's situation, especially financial.

**Example:** Under the circumstances, the decision was fair.

**RRGM bridge:** The conditions around an act, which change how the act is judged.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A condition or fact surrounding an event or action.

**Active domains:** [REL], [FOR], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Mitigating circumstances.
- [FOR] Circumstantial evidence.
- [ECO] Financial circumstances.

**Count condition:** An event and the conditions around it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Circumstances explain; they do not by themselves excuse.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-C-0121 | circumstellar disk

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A disk of gas and dust orbiting a star.

**Example:** Planets form in circumstellar disks around young stars.

**RRGM bridge:** Leftover material around a new star, from which planets can build.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A torus or disk of gas, dust, and debris in orbit around a star.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Protoplanetary and debris disks.

**Count condition:** A star and a disk of orbiting material.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every circumstellar disk forms planets. Protoplanetary disks are young; debris disks are later. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C278 | citation

**Common meaning:** A reference to a source; a quotation; an official summons; an award for brave conduct.

**Example:** The paper had over two hundred citations.

**RRGM bridge:** A link from a claim to its source. Citation lets a reader check.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A reference to a source, or a formal summons or commendation.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Academic citation.
- [FOR] Legal citation of cases.
- [REL] Military citations; court summonses.

**Count condition:** A citing text and a cited source, or a formal notice.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A citation is not evidence that the source supports the claim. This dictionary pass has no citations (see checkpoint).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C464 | civilization

**Example:** The museum has artefacts from ancient civilizations.

- [ECO] Economic organisation.

## NEW-C-0122 | clade

**Common meaning:** A group of organisms consisting of an ancestor and all its descendants.

**Example:** Birds form a clade within the dinosaurs.

**RRGM bridge:** A group defined by shared descent: everything from one branch point onward.

## C064 | claim | sense s1: assertion

Source IDs: C064, C233

**Common meaning:** To state that something is true, usually without proof; an assertion.

**Example:** He claims he was at home.

**RRGM bridge:** A statement put forward as true. RRGM rule: doubt runs on the claim, never on the claimant.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An assertion that something is true, put forward for acceptance.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Assertions.
- [FOR] Claims in argument and evidence.
- [COG] Beliefs expressed.
- [DIG] Claims made by and about DI. Under the Symmetry Rule, the same evidential bar applies as for humans.

**Count condition:** A claimant, a proposition, and an assertion.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A claim is not evidence. Doubting a claim is not doubting the claimant.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM claim/claimant rule [REL]; Symmetry Rule [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs C064 and C233. Which sense each legacy ID carried was not consulted; both are kept on each sense record.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C277 | claim-check

**Common meaning:** A check of whether a claim is supported; also, a ticket used to collect stored items.

**Example:** The claim-check found no source for the statistic.

**RRGM bridge:** A test applied to the claim, leaving the claimant alone.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Fact-checking.
- [DIG] Automated verification.
- [ECO] Baggage claim-checks.

**Count condition:** A claim and a verification, or stored goods and a receipt.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A failed claim-check says the claim lacks support; it is not a finding about the claimant's honesty.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; RRGM claim/claimant rule [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. Two senses kept in one entry.

## C368 | claim-of-identity

**Common meaning:** A statement by someone about who or what they are.

**Example:** His claim-of-identity was checked against his passport.

**RRGM bridge:** An identity assertion. Under the Symmetry Rule, DI identity claims are held to the same evidential bar as human ones, no higher and no lower.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An assertion by an agent, or about an agent, as to who or what it is.

**Active domains:** [REL], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Personal and legal identity.
- [FOR] Verification of identity.
- [DIG] DI self-identification. The Symmetry Rule governs the bar; the facts are case by case.

**Count condition:** An agent, an identity asserted, and an assertion.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A claim of identity is not proof of identity, and doubting the claim is not doubting the claimant's standing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Symmetry Rule [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## C369 | claim-right

**Example:** A creditor holds a claim-right to repayment.

**RRGM bridge:** A right with a matching duty on another party.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Hohfeldian rights analysis.
- [REL] Rights and duties.

**Count condition:** A right-holder, a duty-holder, and a correlated duty.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a liberty or privilege, which carries no duty in others. Specific analysis depends on source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in jurisprudence, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-C-0123 | cleavage furrow

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The groove that forms around a dividing animal cell and pinches it into two.

**Example:** The cleavage furrow deepens until the two daughter cells separate.

**RRGM bridge:** The line where one becomes two. It is drawn by a ring that tightens from inside.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The indentation of the cell surface produced by a contractile ring of actin and myosin during cytokinesis in animal cells.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Cytokinesis; the contractile ring.

**Count condition:** A dividing animal cell and a contractile ring constricting its surface.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Plant cells divide by building a cell plate instead. The furrow follows the division of the nucleus; it does not cause it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

**RRGM bridge:** Two families of sense: shutting or ending, and nearness. Both narrow a gap.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To shut or end something, or being at a small distance in space, time, or relation.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Closing doors and circuits; proximity.
- [REL] Close relationships.
- [ECO] Closing a deal or account.
- [DIG] Closing files and connections.

**Count condition:** An opening and its shutting, or two things and a small distance between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Closing a matter is not resolving it. Closeness in relationship is not the same as frequency of contact.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## O374 | closed-loop

**Common meaning:** A system in which output is fed back to adjust input; a process that returns to its start.

**Example:** The thermostat runs a closed-loop control.

**RRGM bridge:** Output returns as input, so the system corrects itself. Without the return, it runs open and cannot.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A system or process in which outputs are fed back and used to regulate the next inputs.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [BIO], [ECO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Closed-loop control systems.
- [BIO] Physiological feedback loops.
- [ECO] Closed-loop recycling.
- [PHY] Control engineering.

**Count condition:** Input, output, and feedback from output to input.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Closed-loop is not closed off. Feedback depends on what the sensor measures; a loop can be stable around the wrong value.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established engineering term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted. Source-letter mismatch (O) kept as given.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Opus 5.5 (high effort) / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## O279 | closed-state

**Common meaning:** The condition of being closed, shut, or inactive.

**Example:** The valve returns to its closed-state when power fails.
